## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Sixteen-way hosted QEMU backfill

- **Goal and acceptance target:** increase package throughput without weakening source, metadata, checksum, offline-build, upstream-test, or installed-smoke gates. The repository-controlled backfill matrix must admit sixteen independent QEMU package calls; GitHub's account-level hosted-runner quota remains an external queue ceiling and is not represented as a guaranteed capacity.
- **Environment and observed baseline:** the isolated worktree `work/infra-backfill-concurrency-16` starts from protected main `1443971c92a43d353fb0662d3b7c3f476e71a998` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 logical CPUs, and 103 GiB available workspace disk. Backfill `31562621344` still uses the prior eight-way workflow; its latest read showed 18 jobs active across the account while another corrected run remained queued. This observation does not prove a fixed GitHub plan limit.
- **Implementation and safety boundary:** `rpm-repo-backfill.yml` raises `max-parallel` from 8 to 16. Each package still receives a separate hosted runner and immutable commit, image digest, source checksum, supplemental-repository generation, artifact, and failure envelope. The existing three-attempt cache-preserving DNF policy remains the protection for the single official openEuler mirror, and build execution remains offline. Seven observed publishers failed their first payload rsync with exit 12 and the exact forced-command message `Another instance of rrsync is already accessing this directory`; none reached the ready-marker upload. The publisher now retries only that conjunction with bounded exponential delay and deterministic per-package jitter. Authentication, no-overwrite, partial-transfer, 404, checksum, and all other errors remain immediate failures. Payload still precedes `.ready`, preserving atomic server acceptance.
- **Cleanup and validation:** `make clean` isolated the run from stale outputs. Targeted RPM repository tests cover success after lock contention plus negative exit-code/message combinations; fresh `make all` passed repository and metadata validation, the full unit suite, and all three fixed golden packages. Actionlint reported no findings for the changed workflows, Bash syntax passed for the new retry helper, `ci/validate-repository.py --require-published-image` passed, and `git diff --check` was clean.
- **Next action:** publish this infrastructure-only PR through normal protected checks. After merge, start one new protected-main backfill and measure effective running jobs, official-mirror retry rate, QEMU build completion rate, publication wait/failure rate, and public RPM/SRPM growth before considering a return to twenty-way matrix concurrency.
- **Review correction:** PR `#204` review identified that `github.run_id` alone was identical for all 16 matrix calls and could synchronize retry waves. The bounded retry now hashes `run_id:package_id` again for each attempt, so independent publishers receive package-specific delay sequences while authentication, no-overwrite, checksum, HTTP, and every non-exact lock failure still fail immediately. A regression requires the per-package key and rejects a return to the shared run-only seed.

## 2026-08-11 — Codex — Per-package rpmbuild user-policy mechanism

- **Goal and evidence:** fix the shared build-identity defect demonstrated by FLAC PR `#158` exact head `2f51880df9e60ce39f0500eb009176617de88473`, Package CI run `31486213688`. Independently preserved source/build/smoke artifacts matched their Actions digests, were schema/head/target/image/source validated, and showed ten CTest cases with eight passing and only `libFLAC`/`libFLAC++` failing after 5,205.675 seconds. Their first effective error, `iterator claims file is writable when tester thinks it should not be; are you running as root?`, proves that root bypassed the read-only chmod semantics exercised by the unchanged upstream tests. This is a shared user-model defect, not RISC-V, QEMU, or a safe FLAC source/SPEC repair.
- **Compatibility boundary:** `build.user` is now an optional, schema-enumerated per-package policy accepting only `root` and `unprivileged`. Absence means `root`, preserving every existing package and root-capability suite such as ACL/attr. `needs-native-riscv` packages continue to bypass both dependency and QEMU build paths. Only an explicit package-only `unprivileged` opt-in provisions and selects the fixed `rpmbuild` identity `10001:10001`; no package directory is changed by this infrastructure commit.
- **Implementation:** the digest-pinned base image and audited BuildRequires resolution are unchanged. Dependency planning and DNF installation now state UID/GID `0:0` explicitly and record that identity. For an unprivileged opt-in, the unpublished per-run derived image receives the fixed user after dependency installation. The offline build orchestrator validates the exact 40-hex head, local derived-image tag, canonical package/work/artifact paths, read-only repository mount, and `--network none`; a root-only preparation call rejects symlink entries and hands the fresh generated work tree to `10001:10001`, then the target call verifies its effective identity and executes unchanged `scripts/build-rpm --offline`. Root policy uses the same orchestrator with UID/GID `0:0` and the historical artifact location.
- **Artifact and provenance contract:** both policies still write `rpmbuild-phase-result.json` bound to the explicit PR head, preserve the outer and internal logs, and retain RPMS/SRPMS in the existing work trees. The unprivileged path copies only three regular JSON records from its target-owned result directory into host evidence. The existing allowlist stager still archives only regular JSON/log files and regular RPM products; a new regression proves a target-created read-only RPM remains host-stageable. The build exit code, safe artifact staging, seven-day retention, pinned OCI digest, source/build network split, and final envelope paths remain unchanged.
- **Environment and isolation:** work used branch `infra/rootless-rpmbuild-target-user` in isolated worktree `work/infra-rootless-rpmbuild-target-user`, rebased onto anonymously fetched protected main `253665a4bf77edebfdcc7187dc8026e292d4962b`. Host `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` ran macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64 with 8 CPUs, approximately 16 GiB memory and 105 GiB free disk; Git 2.50.1, Python 3.9.6, Actionlint and ShellCheck were present. Docker, Podman, QEMU, RPM, and rpmbuild were absent, so no target-container or FLAC success is claimed.
- **Cleanup, commands, and results:** final generated evidence used the fresh ignored directory `work/infra-rootless-build-user-validation-postrebase-20260811`. An initial attempt to wrap validation with `ci/run-capped.sh` failed locally because macOS lacks GNU `timeout`; that failed attempt is retained separately and was not used as success evidence. Fresh post-rebase `make validate` accepted 112/112 package manifests and 3/3 golden manifests; static repository validation passed with zero errors/warnings and a published digest lock. Full `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests -v` passed 51/51, including schema/default/invalid-policy, UID/GID, symlink-safe ownership, root dependency, native routing, exact-head/offline command, structured evidence copy, and target-owned RPM regressions. Golden materialization passed 3/3; Actionlint over all workflows passed; the local-only credential guard passed with authentication mode `none` and `token_value_recorded=false`; `git diff --check` and the no-`packages/` scope guard passed.
- **Migration, rollback, and residual risk:** merge the infrastructure mechanism without opting in any package, then add `build.user: unprivileged` to FLAC in a separate package-only PR and require its unchanged ten-test suite plus RPM/install smoke to pass on the exact head. Fixed numeric identity collision, Linux bind-mount ownership behavior, user creation in the target image, and QEMU runtime remain unverified locally and therefore fail closed in remote CI. If the opt-in path regresses, remove only the package metadata opt-in to return to the compatible root behavior; if the mechanism itself is defective, revert this infrastructure commit. Root-required suites must never be migrated merely to make CI green, and no test may be skipped or privilege-wrapped to simulate success.

## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Read-only 50-host audit and commit-ready Runner fleet tooling

- **Environment and evidence:** the isolated implementation worktree is `work/infra-runner-fleet` on branch `infra/runner-fleet`, based on commit `31db527990fb5d971f4710bcce2bd3768c6af14b`; unrelated workflow/validator edits from the coordinating infrastructure task were preserved. The read-only fleet audit evidence is under `work/runner-fleet-audit-20260812/run-20260812T060732Z`: all 50 addresses `.201-.250` were reachable, all ran Ubuntu 26.04 with kernel `7.0.0-27`, 32 logical AMD EPYC 9654 CPUs, approximately 60.8 GiB RAM, and approximately 951 GiB free beneath `/opt`. Forty-six hosts were `systemd=running`; `.211`, `.220`, `.224`, and `.231` were degraded only by `fwupd-refresh.service`. No host had Docker, Podman, QEMU/binfmt, or an Actions Runner. A low-concurrency repeat reached `github.com`, `api.github.com`, `ghcr.io`, and `repo.openeuler.org` from all 50 hosts. No audit command installed, wrote, restarted, registered, or removed anything.
- **Implementation:** `ops/actions-runner-fleet` now contains an explicit fleet registry, fail-closed root policy, pinned `actions/runner` v2.336.0 URL/SHA-256/226035903-byte size lock, idempotent installer, process-only credential bridge, separate offline registration and reviewed activation, systemd sandbox, preflight/audit/uninstall tools, synchronous job guard/hooks, fleet audit helper, and operator documentation. Ubuntu packages provide Docker plus `qemu-user`/`qemu-user-binfmt`. Registration tokens are accepted only from one stdin line or process environment, long-lived PAT prefixes and credential arguments are rejected, Runner default labels are retained, and no secret is written to a file, command line, service environment, test fixture, or log. Activation requires `--enable-reviewed-policy`; if its preflight or service start fails after switching policy false→true, the EXIT guard stops/disables the service and atomically restores false. The four conditional hosts persist their separately reviewed `ALLOW_DEGRADED` decision in root-owned identity, and every later lifecycle command plus systemd preflight consumes that exact immutable value.
- **Dedicated-host cleanup contract:** after exact host/name/identity/path guards and a per-runner lock, cleanup observes Docker twice before mutation and fails closed if any running container exists. On an idle, single-runner host it uses bounded inventories to remove all stopped/created containers, dangling volumes, unused custom networks, and only `openeuler-builddeps:*` derived images. It never performs global image/build-cache pruning, so the digest-pinned GHCR base image remains cached. Workspace, temporary home, and Docker-client state deletion is constrained to verified managed directories. A five-minute hook timeout and 512-object bound prevent unbounded cleanup.
- **Remote repository security state:** a separate authenticated Actions-settings operation verified that the repository is public, `yinjiayi` is its only collaborator and has admin access, and it had zero self-hosted runners. Fork-workflow approval was changed from `first_time_contributors` to `all_external_contributors` and independently read back. The command category was repository Actions-settings read/update; no credential value was recorded. The precise rollback setting is `first_time_contributors`. This setting is defense in depth and does not replace the protected-main Runner gate.
- **Cleanup and validation:** no generated remote state existed because deployment was intentionally not run. Local validation used the isolated worktree and temporary per-test fake Docker executables/logs that were deleted automatically. `bash -n` passed every fleet shell entry point; the executable shell guard test passed; ShellCheck passed with sourced-library analysis; the 12 focused static/behavior tests passed, including running-container no-mutation, stopped-container/dangling-volume/unused-network cleanup, derived-image-only deletion, retained GHCR cache, fixed policy/workflow/event/label routes, persisted conditional-host policy, activation rollback, exact release locks, and absence of credential-shaped literals. The full repository suite passed 76/76 tests, repository validation passed, and `git diff --check` passed. These are local code/static results only; Docker/QEMU installation, Runner enrollment, live workload isolation, and throughput remain unverified because no remote write was authorized or performed.
- **Rollout and next action:** publish the tooling with the coordinating protected infrastructure PR, then separately review branch protection and the two exact main workflow revisions. Only after that review, install/register/activate `.201-.205` as five canaries, one Runner each, and record service health, job isolation, CPU/memory/disk growth, QEMU throughput, Docker cleanup, queue latency, and network failures. Expand to the remaining 41 clean hosts only after the canary gate passes; handle the four degraded hosts in a separate decision using `--allow-degraded`. Roll back a failed canary by disabling the service, using a fresh short-lived removal token through stdin, and running the scoped uninstaller; Docker/QEMU packages are intentionally retained.

## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Canary activation failed closed on a missing systemd Netlink allowance

- **Goal and exact operation:** activate only `10.230.50.201` after its successful offline registration, while leaving `.202-.205` untouched. The command performed one idle cleanup, called exact protected-main `activate.sh --enable-reviewed-policy`, and required an immediate repository audit. Evidence is `/private/tmp/runner-canary-deploy.Bg0u7t/merge-b1dc260e-20260812T0724Z/activation-201`.
- **Observed result and rollback:** root cleanup completed twice with no Docker objects to remove, and the explicit pre-service preflight passed. The systemd instance then failed its `ExecStartPre`. The activation EXIT guard stopped and disabled the service and atomically restored `OE_RUNNER_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED=false`. Independent post-failure audit confirms registration remains true while policy, service enablement, and service activity are false. GitHub therefore continues to see `.201` offline, and the other four were never activated.
- **Root cause:** the unit limits socket families to `AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6`. Inside that sandbox, `preflight.sh` calls `oe_assert_local_host`, whose `ip -o -4 address show scope global` uses the kernel's rtnetlink interface. Since `AF_NETLINK` was absent, the command yielded no address and the journal recorded `this machine does not own expected address 10.230.50.201`. The same preflight passed outside the unit, explaining the context-specific difference. This is not an IP, QEMU, Docker, credential, or GitHub registration defect.
- **Repair and security boundary:** add only `AF_NETLINK` to `RestrictAddressFamilies`; do not add `AF_PACKET`, raw sockets, capabilities, or broader service privileges. A static regression binds this allowance to the existing `ip address` identity check and rejects packet/raw families. Publish through normal protected checks, deploy the exact resulting main commit to the five still-offline canaries, and retry activation on `.201` only before touching `.202-.205`.
- **Live A/B confirmation:** two bounded, automatically collected transient systemd units on `.201` ran the exact address predicate as `oegha`. The control with `AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6` returned 1 and emitted `Cannot open netlink socket: Address family not supported by protocol`; the otherwise identical unit with only `AF_NETLINK` added returned 0. The probe then rechecked runner policy false and service inactive. Evidence is `/private/tmp/runner-canary-deploy.Bg0u7t/merge-b1dc260e-20260812T0724Z/activation-201/netlink-ab.{stdout,stderr}`. This proves the narrow family addition is both necessary and sufficient for the observed preflight failure without broadening packet/raw access.

## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Same-second Runner diagnostic ownership collision after Netlink repair

- **Goal and protected input:** after PR #240 passed required checks and merged as protected main `78a1eb506aa81d5f49cfb63b4a89d85917d568b3`, create a new archive at `/private/tmp/runner-canary-deploy.Bg0u7t/merge-78a1eb50-20260812T0737Z/runner-fleet-78a1eb50.tar` (SHA-256 `92aa900cff7cbb0012d9467986a6a0d0ac70aadd7c1cd7c8983f4164edca00d1`), update the five registered/offline canaries, and retry only `.201`. The update audit passed 5/5 with registration true and policy/service false before activation.
- **Observed failure and rollback:** `.201` cleanup and explicit preflight passed, then systemd `ExecStartPre` failed again. The rollback stopped/disabled the service and restored policy false. Exact journal evidence records `UnauthorizedAccessException` opening `_diag/Runner_20260812-073737-utc.log`, followed by the wrapper's version mismatch. Post-failure audit again reports registered true, policy false, and service disabled/inactive; `.202-.205` were not activated.
- **Root cause evidence:** `_diag` is correctly `oegha:oegha:0700`; a direct `oegha` probe and a transient unit with the full sandbox both successfully created files and ran Runner 2.336.0. The exact failed-second log is instead `root:root:0644`. Seven historic root-owned logs correlate with root lifecycle version probes, while normal Runner/registration logs are `oegha`-owned. `Runner.Listener --version` always opens a timestamped, second-granularity diagnostic log. `activate.sh` invokes root-side preflight immediately before systemd invokes the same probe as `oegha`; when both occur in one second, the service account tries to reopen the root-created name and receives EACCES. This is not a sandbox write-path or directory-owner defect.
- **Repair boundary:** introduce one installed-payload version helper that, whenever called by root, runs `Runner.Listener --version` as the fixed `oegha` service account from the canonical Runner directory. `install.sh`, `register.sh`, `audit.sh`, and `preflight.sh` all use it; non-root service preflight executes directly. Extraction-time validation remains root-local because it precedes the managed runtime tree and cannot collide with systemd. Do not chown diagnostic history, make `_diag` group/world writable, delay arbitrarily, or weaken the version check. Add static regression over all four lifecycle scripts, run full validation, then publish a separate protected repair and retry only `.201`.

## 2026-08-14 — Codex — Self-hosted unprivileged build readback repair

- **Goal and hypothesis:** repair the protected-main artifact gap that remained after input access PR #376. The dedicated Runner service intentionally uses `UMask=0077`; after the root preparation container recursively handed `work/<package>` to UID/GID `10001:10001`, those pre-existing `0700`/`0600` modes prevented the host `oegha` process from reading `.ci-result`, `RPMS`, and `SRPMS`. - **Target and environment:** isolated worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-unprivileged-readback.wdq5SS/worktree`, branch `infra/unprivileged-build-readback-20260814`, exact protected-main base `f1c20c3bb469ce02d7b485d8e6c29764405846d9`. Local validation ran on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` (Darwin 25.5.0 arm64, 8 CPUs, approximately 84 GiB free). GitHub reported all 50 repository runners online and idle. Exact failure evidence came from protected-main Package CI `31787121236` on `oe-rva23-qemu-236` / `RISC-V-Github-Runner-36` (Ubuntu 26.04, Docker 29.1.3): the container step returned success, but artifact `9214093240` omitted `ownership-handoff.json`, `build-identity.json`, `rpmbuild-phase-result.json`, and all RPM/SRPM outputs; downstream smoke failed because the binary RPM directory was missing. The corresponding GitHub-hosted PR run produced a result directory with mode `0755`, isolating the service umask as the host-specific difference.
- **Cleanup and commands:** work began from a fresh `git worktree` at protected main. Validation output was isolated under `/private/tmp/unprivileged-readback-validation.FLYS3u`; `make clean` ran before `make validate`, `make test`, and `make golden`. No local RPM, Docker, QEMU, or target build was run, following the user-approved CI-first policy.
- **Implementation:** ownership handoff now validates the complete non-symlink work tree, removes any other-write bit, grants other-read on regular files and other-read/search on directories, then changes ownership to the fixed build UID. This keeps the build writable only by UID 10001 while allowing the host Runner to collect public evidence and RPMs after the container exits. Successful unprivileged builds must expose all three required evidence files; missing, unreadable, symlinked, or non-regular evidence now fails closed instead of silently producing an empty success artifact. Root build policy and the service `UMask=0077` remain unchanged.
- **Observed results and limitations:** repository validation passed with no warnings, all 103 unit tests passed, golden materialization was valid, and the new regression reproduces restrictive `0700`/`0600` work-tree modes while proving the exact host readback bits and missing-evidence failure. These are static and host-policy results only; definitive acceptance still requires an exact protected-main `workflow_dispatch` on the self-hosted pool after this infrastructure PR merges.
- **Concurrent package progress:** libasyncns PR #391 passed networked target build and smoke and merged as `b33b498e4db600ab4dd3dea687647aab7cde7e9f`. The obsolete offline-network holds on libmodbus #345 and liblo #338 were removed only after exact networked Package CI runs `31761326415` and `31766515355` passed all required gates; both strict six-file PRs then squash-merged as `9445b44138c3f1d4d183638e6babdf6d1d45b62d` and `f1c20c3bb469ce02d7b485d8e6c29764405846d9`. The Silver Searcher #394 second repair remains bound to CI head `c5a322cbc7638d0988089b6b9e4f0ec1713d9f12`; no terminal success is claimed yet.
- **Next action:** publish this four-file infrastructure change, require exact-head CI, and merge it separately from package work. Then dispatch one protected-main unprivileged package canary with publication disabled; accept the repair only if build evidence and RPMs are present, installation smoke passes, and no runner cleanup anomaly appears. Re-check the supplemental repository endpoint before any publish-enabled backfill.

## 2026-08-14 — Codex — Trusted PR dispatch and 48-way fleet concurrency

- **Goal and hypothesis:** raise useful package throughput on the fifty-host x86_64/QEMU fleet while preserving the protected-main trust boundary. A maintainer-local dispatcher should be able to bind one exact internal package PR head to a `workflow_dispatch` run, but the workflow must repeat that authorization server-side before any caller-selected commit is checked out. Repository backfill concurrency should increase from the fallback of 32 to 48, retaining two idle workers for repair canaries.
- **Target and environment:** work used isolated branch `infra/trusted-pr-qemu-dispatch-20260814` at protected-main base `17368bdf4ccdde1d5aabdcdae93c8b2dc1f5cf68` in `/private/tmp/openeuler-trusted-qemu-dispatch-20260814`; the dirty user worktree and its candidate packages were not modified. Local validation ran on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, about 83 GiB free disk, Python 3.9.6, GitHub CLI 2.97.0, and authenticated keyring user `yinjiayi`. GitHub reported `oe-rva23-qemu-201` through `-250` online, idle, Linux/X64, and labeled `trusted-main-only`. Direct read-only SSH probes of `.201`, `.225`, and `.250` could not authenticate with the current local key, so this round relies on the prior recorded 32-vCPU/~60.8-GiB/~951-GiB-free fleet audit plus current GitHub service state rather than claiming a fresh host-shell audit.
- **Security finding and implementation:** review found that a local-only guard was insufficient because a direct manual dispatch could otherwise supply arbitrary `commit_sha` and `publish_to_repo=true`. `Package CI` now adds a GitHub-hosted authorization job that first checks out only `github.sha` from protected `main`, then verifies the exact open PR number, same-repository head, trusted author/association, allowed branch prefix, current base/head SHA, one-package file scope, protected-main event ref, and publication disabled. Every job that can check out the PR head depends on the authorization output. The maintainer dispatcher repeats the same checks locally, uses an unambiguous PR/SHA run name, downloads the final artifact, verifies package/SHA/status binding, rechecks the live PR, and only then posts required status contexts. Manual main rebuilds and protected `workflow_call` backfills retain their existing publication paths; PR-head dispatch cannot publish.
- **Concurrency and network policy:** after credential preflight, repository Actions variable `RPM_BACKFILL_MAX_CONCURRENCY` was created as `48` and read back at `2026-08-14T15:46:20Z`; no backfill was started by that mutation. The target build remains network-enabled through `ci/build-config.yaml` and may retrieve pinned sources and dependencies online, with SHA-256 verification before rpmbuild. This work did not add an offline-build requirement and did not run a local RPM, Docker, or QEMU target build.
- **Cleanup, commands, and results:** `make clean` removed stale `work/local-validation`, followed by `make validate`, `make test`, `make golden`, focused trusted-dispatch tests, Python bytecode compilation, Actionlint, Ruby YAML parsing, credential guards, and `git diff --check`. Repository validation and golden materialization passed; the complete suite passed 108/108 tests and the focused suite passed 5/5. Generated validation evidence remains under `work/local-validation`; credential output is `/private/tmp/infra-trusted-dispatch-preflight-20260814.json` and records no token value.
- **Concurrent package result:** sed PR #155 was repaired in isolated worktree `/private/tmp/sed-pr155-repair-20260814`. Commit `32002121a40c83b8f6c7f36cc99918a2cdf497e6` removes fragile generated `configure`/m4 patch hunks, forces the gnulib replacement through `gl_cv_func_localeconv_works=no`, preserves `%make_build check`, and retains the one-line `lib/localeconv.c` normalization. Fresh source verification and GNU patch 2.8 with `--fuzz=0` passed, as did 101 repository tests and golden validation; no local target build ran. Package CI `31815695743` is in progress, so target success is not claimed. Dos2unix run `31814437243` passed; less run `31814137546` failed and remains under separate exact-artifact diagnosis.
- **Next action:** publish this infrastructure-only PR, require its exact-head hosted checks, merge normally, then run one publication-disabled trusted-dispatch canary and confirm a named `oe-rva23-qemu-*` worker plus exact build-result evidence. Only after that canary should independent QEMU-buildable package PRs be dispatched concurrently, capped below the 48-worker backfill ceiling; any failed package remains a package-only repair with a fresh lease and exact-head evidence.
- **Follow-up caller-context guard:** reusable workflows inherit the caller `github` context, so an RPM backfill started by `workflow_dispatch` also carries a nonempty `commit_sha`. The final authorization and publication gates therefore allow that path only when `github.workflow_ref` is exactly `yinjiayi/openeuler-riscv-packages/.github/workflows/rpm-repo-backfill.yml@refs/heads/main`; arbitrary reusable callers and direct Package CI dispatches cannot claim it. Static checks and the full local suite must pass before publication.

## 2026-08-24 — Codex — Full inventory Dashboard and evidence-bound RPM links

- **Goal and acceptance target:** make the complete package inventory the Dashboard's primary view, assign one explicit lifecycle status to every inventory key, and expose direct RPM/SRPM URLs only when immutable repository publication is proven. Acceptance required all committed inventory keys to remain unique and visible, browser search/filter/pagination over the complete set, matching Dashboard schemas, and no constructed package URL without a same-package/same-commit/same-generation upload and verification pair.
- **Target and environment:** isolated infrastructure branch `infra/dashboard-package-inventory-20260824` in `/private/tmp/dashboard-package-inventory.OkfQNd`, rebased onto `origin/main` `f1c1dde08a8c028546201082daa2beb16de5ce51`; original dirty package worktree preserved. Local host `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` (`10.213.0.254`), macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 logical CPUs, 16 GiB installed memory, approximately 100 GiB free disk, Python 3.9.6, Git 2.50.1, GitHub CLI 2.97.0, and Make 3.81. No local RPM, Docker, QEMU, or target build ran.
- **Inputs and definitions:** primary inventory input remained committed `catalog/package-index.json.gz`, generated at `2026-08-23T12:43:41Z` from snapshot `discovery-20260808T165000Z-9a89920c269462cd`. The browser list overlays current managed metadata, current PR facts when supplied, exact-head CI result envelopes, and retained publication evidence. `build-succeeded` requires a successful result bound to a recorded package/PR commit. `published` additionally requires paired `rpm-repository-upload-batch` and passed `rpm-repository-publication-verification` documents with identical package, commit, and generation; only that state receives direct `riscv64/Packages/*.rpm` and `source/Packages/*.rpm` URLs.
- **Implementation:** `scripts/generate-dashboard` now emits the summary/managed `data.json` plus compact full `inventory.json`, overlays current managed metadata without mutating the source inventory, filters malformed upstream URLs, and omits empty per-entry fields. The primary table searches names, aliases, component IDs, and decision labels, filters by status/evidence, and renders 50/100/250-row pages. The managed detail view remains below update health. `ci/collect-dashboard-evidence.py` downloads retained smoke/publication JSON from independent GitHub artifacts with eight concurrent workers and safe regular-JSON extraction; the Dashboard workflow passes those facts to the generator. Two schemas distinguish the summary and full-list payloads.
- **Cleanup and validation:** each evidence-producing round began with `make clean`; the final route ran `make validate`, `make test`, `make golden`, and `make dashboard`, then schema reconciliation, uniqueness/status/link checks, `git diff --check`, and browser QA through a temporary `127.0.0.1` server. All 111 unit tests passed, metadata/repository/package-index validation passed, golden materialization succeeded, and both Dashboard schemas returned zero errors. The generated list has 151,852 entries and 151,852 unique `inventory_id` values; metadata and calculated status counts reconcile exactly. No row had only one of RPM/SRPM, and no non-`published` row had a repository product link. Compact `inventory.json` is 37,171,259 bytes.
- **Browser QA:** the desktop view loaded all summary facts and rendered rows 1–100 of 151,852; `utf8cpp` search returned exactly the managed package plus `utf8cpp-git`; managed-status pagination advanced from rows 1–100 to 101–200 of 263; a published-evidence filter correctly returned zero for the local generation because no live CI evidence directory was supplied. At a 390×844 viewport, document width equaled viewport width, the filter stack fit within the section, 100 rows remained paginated inside the horizontal table wrapper, and no browser console warnings/errors appeared. A missing favicon produced one harmless local-server 404 and did not affect the application.
- **Failures and limitations:** focused tests must run through discovery because direct module import does not place `scripts/tests/helpers.py` on Python's import path; the initial direct invocation failed before executing tests. The first discovery run exposed a ZIP regular-file mode edge case; the collector was corrected and focused tests passed. The first complete suite then caught an assertion that expected omitted empty RPM keys; the test was corrected to the compact optional-field contract and the full suite passed. An optional `node --check` could not run because this host has no standalone Node CLI; the in-app browser nevertheless executed `dashboard/app.js` without syntax/runtime errors. The local generated Dashboard legitimately reports zero published/build-succeeded entries because `make dashboard` has no retained Actions artifacts; it does not claim the supplemental repository is empty. The live workflow can show only evidence still available through GitHub's seven-day artifact retention, so historical packages without retained verification remain at a weaker truthful status. The repository-server outage recorded above was not reclassified or retried.
- **Next action:** publish this infrastructure-only branch, require protected CI and a successful Dashboard workflow, then verify that its evidence collector finds retained artifacts and that every displayed RPM/SRPM URL resolves to the exact immutable generation when the supplemental endpoint is healthy. After merge, resume one-package onboarding PRs independently; refresh the committed package inventory in its own generated-data milestone when current PR/main drift materially changes the source snapshot readback.

The authenticated GitHub CLI session was `yinjiayi`; the pasted PAT was never used or recorded.

Existing dirty user work and its untracked package directories were preserved. Source review used GitHub GraphQL metadata plus fresh HTTPS tag-archive downloads, SHA-256 calculation, traversal/symlink/member-count/expanded-size checks, and build/license inspection. Each wave ran `make clean`, `make validate`, `make test`, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, `git diff --check`, and `scripts/github-credential-guard --local-only --require-auth`. No local RPM, QEMU, or target-container build ran; CI may fetch the pinned source and dependencies over the network.

Every PR carries only its package directory, labels `operation:onboard` and `source:supplementary`, and squash auto-merge. All five aggregate validations passed; each unit round passed 112/112 tests and both golden and Dashboard generation completed. The fourth wave initially exposed an invalid underscore package ID and an overly broad local synchronization that removed tracked package directories only inside its disposable worktree; tracked files were restored before commit, stale generated directories were isolated, the invalid candidate was replaced, and the complete validation reran successfully.
- **Transient failures and recovery:** several archive downloads returned TLS EOF; each was retried against the same official URL and accepted only when its SHA-256 exactly matched the independently observed digest. One PR-creation GraphQL request and several branch pushes failed transiently; idempotent lookup/retry recovered them without force-push. Credential preflight passed before every commit/push/PR/auto-merge mutation group and recorded no token value.

Those counts were an intermediate observation, not a terminal build result. Later waves entered the same CI queue. No RPM or SRPM repository URL is claimed from these PRs, and no package is marked published without matching immutable upload and verification evidence.
- **Next action:** continue the sixth and later waves at 100 packages each, preserving the same source and isolation gates. Monitor exact-head CI in parallel, repair package-specific BuildRequires/spec/test failures on their own PRs, and allow only successful protected checks to auto-merge. Refresh the full inventory and Dashboard after material PR/main drift, and expose RPM/SRPM URLs only for evidence-bound immutable publications.

## 2026-08-27 — Codex — Open-PR CI parser repair and queue recovery

- **Goal, hypothesis, and acceptance boundary:** concentrate on current open-package PR failures before onboarding more inventory. The initial hypothesis was that a shared generated-SPEC defect could explain the unusually uniform failure set. Acceptance for this round required an exact current-head audit, a minimal package-local repair with no force-push, remote SHA verification, canary evidence that reached beyond the former failure point, and truthful separation of later dependency/build failures. A **queue recovery** here means cancelling only not-yet-started, exact-head PR runs made redundant by controlled repair verification; it does not delete artifacts, close PRs, or count cancelled checks as passing.
- **Environment and repository state:** work ran on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB memory, and approximately 67 GiB free disk. The target remained openEuler 24.03 LTS SP3 `riscv64`/RVA23. The audit worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-batch029o-20260827.9B0239/repo` retained its local aggregate staging commit and remained isolated from the user's dirty validation worktree. Remote protected main was `7bfb6b9abeb4a0d86c725273c3c8a4fcb2e803ac`. Local `gh` authentication had drifted to another saved account; `gh auth switch` restored the active account to `yinjiayi`, and every subsequent mutation group passed `scripts/github-credential-guard --local-only --require-auth` without recording a token.
- **Initial CI evidence and root cause:** a fresh open-PR snapshot contained 1,493 PRs, including 1,451 package PRs in the first audit. Among current heads represented by the latest 1,000 Package CI runs, 873 had failed, 28 had `startup_failure`, 49 were queued, and 501 older open-package heads were outside that API window. Structured build artifacts from `dtkmultimedia`, `linux-minesweeper`, and `deepin-font-manager` agreed: metadata, source, patch, repository, merge-policy, and authorization gates passed, then `rpmbuild` rejected the SPEC before a real build with `%P: argument expected`. Generated SPECs contained `find ... -printf '/%P\n'`; RPM requires the literal percent to be escaped as `-printf '/%%P\n'`. The earlier generic `upstream-build` labels were therefore inaccurate for this signature.
- **Exact repair set and validation:** the final fresh open-PR audit identified 1,366 normal `onboard/inventory-*` heads with exactly one bad line and no escaped line, 59 unaffected inventory heads, and 26 early/manual branches with different naming or implementation that were explicitly excluded. A deterministic temporary-index helper created 1,366 unique strict fast-forward commits, each changing exactly one `packages/<id>/<id>.spec`, replacing one `%P` occurrence, passing `git diff --check`, and verifying one escaped occurrence. Two canary worktrees additionally passed fresh `make clean`, `make validate`, and `make golden` before push.
- **Remote mutation and transport anomaly:** the first HTTPS pass atomically pushed and verified 1,050 / 1,366 branches in groups of 50, then `git ls-remote` failed before the next group with `LibreSSL SSL_connect: SSL_ERROR_SYSCALL`; no partial group was pushed. GitHub SSH on port 22 was unavailable, while authenticated `ssh.github.com:443` succeeded for `yinjiayi`. The resumable verifier rechecked the first 1,050 heads, atomically pushed the remaining 316 as six groups of 50 plus one group of 16, and finished at 1,366 / 1,366 with no stale head, force-push, or drift. A fresh PR read then found all 1,366 still-open repair records at their exact new SHA, zero at an old SHA, and zero unexpected heads.
- **Canary result and next failure layer:** repaired canary runs `33044230502` and `33044229868` no longer failed during immediate SPEC parsing. Both installed audited BuildRequires for roughly 16–20 minutes and reached the real `Build SRPM and RPM with verified source networking` step. They then failed in CMake because Qt development dependencies were absent from their SPECs. The immutable official repository metadata at revision `1772729228` was downloaded from the configured RVA23 URL; compressed primary SQLite SHA-256 `7023e5bb09a3a268acc6a411801bfabaf282d8569371f24cd63ede54f6c1dfbc` matched `repomd.xml`. It proves `qt6-qtbase-devel` and `qt6-qtmultimedia-devel` exist for riscv64. PR `#1907` therefore gained those two exact BuildRequires at commit `43ec1b7f686f99623b823f755fcf900e7af8a051`, after static validation and a credential-guarded strict fast-forward push. Protected-main exact-head dispatch run `33046395018` was then requested with publication disabled. `dtkmultimedia` was not given a speculative partial fix: its upstream build also requires DTK6, ncnn, opencv-mobile, mpv, and related development packages absent from the current official primary metadata, so it remains a dependency-chain blocker rather than a claimed success.
- **Queue recovery and runner evidence:** all 50 repository self-hosted runners were online and idle, but local rules intentionally prohibit PR and merge-queue jobs from using them; only heavy QEMU build/smoke from protected main or an authorized exact-head dispatch may use that pool after a GitHub-hosted scope gate. The 1,366 repair synchronizations created a GitHub-hosted gate backlog. A two-window API snapshot identified exactly 1,350 target repair runs still `queued`, 15 already started, and no unrelated event in the cancellation plan. Ordinary cancellation returned accepted for all 1,350 but did not change authoritative state. A one-run `force-cancel` canary reached `completed/cancelled`; the same exact plan then returned 1,349 force-cancel requests plus that one already-terminal result, with zero errors. Package CI queued count fell from 1,404 to 63 and then 57; artifacts and PRs were preserved. Six retained samples reached `rpmbuild-riscv64`. Exact-head dispatch `33046395018` then passed authorization, scope, metadata, repository, source, merge-policy, and patch gates; its heavy build started on self-hosted runner `oe-rva23-qemu-215` and entered audited BuildRequires installation with 49 other fleet hosts idle.
- **Outputs, limitation, and next action:** durable local audit/cancellation records are `/private/tmp/openeuler-bad-find-printf-open-prs-final-20260827.json`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-find-printf-repair-commits-20260827.json`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-find-printf-repair-pushes-20260827.json`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-package-ci-cancel-plan-20260827.json`, and `/private/tmp/openeuler-package-ci-force-cancellations-20260827.json`. ## 2026-08-30 — Codex — root-build workspace traversal repair

- **Goal and exact failure evidence:** repair the shared host-side workspace permissions exposed by ACL PR #447 without making a read-only container bind mount writable or weakening the package test. Exact run `33287903619` attempt 1 at head `5b1e9b185fb6d08a9aa836a3546895307cc10bb8` stopped on a transient Savannah HTTP 502. Its single bounded rerun reached `%check`, where the package tried to run `chmod a+x /workspace /workspace/work` and failed because `/workspace` is intentionally mounted read-only. Root builds can launch unprivileged test identities such as `bin`; those identities need search permission on the fixed mount parents before they can reach the build tree, but they do not need to list or read those parents.
- **Environment and cleanup:** work used isolated worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-ci-root-traversal-phase21.yHDQh3/worktree` from protected `main` `fa87ec345917e7c73b737e344d2fbd03dcb1be65` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and 43 GiB free disk, with Git 2.50.1 and GitHub CLI 2.97.0 authenticated locally as `yinjiayi`. `make clean` isolated the generated validation state before the test round. No local RPM, QEMU, Docker, target build, deployment, or repository publication ran.
- **Change and security boundary:** `ci/run-rpmbuild-container.py` now grants other-users execute-only mode `001` to the repository root and its fixed `work` parent only for root-build mode. Package sources, the package work directory, and artifact directories keep their existing `000` other-user bits; the helper also retains the real-directory checks and never follows a substituted symlink. A focused regression test asserts both the two allowed traversal bits and the protected descendants.
- **Acceptance evidence:** `python3 -m unittest scripts.tests.test_ci_build_user.BuildContainerCommandTests.test_root_build_exposes_only_workspace_parent_traversal scripts.tests.test_ci_build_user.BuildContainerCommandTests.test_non_root_build_handoff_exposes_only_owned_trees` passed; `make validate`, `make test` (114/114), `make golden`, and `git diff --check` also passed after a clean validation round.
- **Result and publication boundary:** this infrastructure repair does not itself prove ACL package success and produces no RPM or SRPM. After the infrastructure PR merges, remove the now-invalid container-side parent `chmod` from the ACL SPEC, validate and fast-forward the exact package PR head, then run one protected exact-head dispatch. Expose product URLs only if a matching immutable supplemental-repository generation is uploaded and verified.

The next guard invocation, placed immediately before PR creation, returned a transient authentication error; because that shell group did not yet use fail-fast chaining, the following `gh pr create` nevertheless created PR #1963. The anomaly was detected from the same command output, a standalone bounded guard retry passed, and every later mutation used `set -euo pipefail`; squash auto-merge was enabled only after that successful guard. No credential value was recorded or exposed.
- **Fleet anomaly and truthful boundary:** several concurrently sampled QEMU jobs failed before any workflow step on runners including `oe-rva23-qemu-223`, `-224`, `-227`, `-229`, `-230`, `-240`, `-242`, `-244`, and `-250` because the installed job hook detected a running container and refused every cleanup mutation. This is fail-closed host state, not package evidence; old `artifacts/build` files exposed after setup failure are stale and must not be classified as the current package. The overlay does not repair or conceal that host state, and no package success or RPM/SRPM URL is inferred.
- **Next action:** submit and squash-merge this isolated infrastructure change through hosted checks, then resume exact-head dispatches in package-ID-isolated groups after the orphaned containers have exited or an independently audited fleet cleanup has restored idle hosts. After `make clean`, `make validate`, all 123 tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, the nine-test focused overlay suite, actionlint, `git diff --check`, and the authenticated local credential guard all pass. The focused cases cover ordinary checkout, separate tooling/package heads, evidence identity/tree tampering, missing paired inputs, tracked tampering, untracked injection, schema strictness, and seven-job workflow wiring. This hosted scope/provenance defect is not a libcap-ng package failure, QEMU result, build success, or publication result. No RPM or SRPM exists from the failed canary and no repository URL is claimed.

## 2026-08-31 — Codex — dependency-timeout classification and fleet-state follow-up

- **Goal, hypothesis, and scope:** continue the third fifty-PR cohort by separating deterministic package failures from old-workflow timeouts and stale Runner state. Exact run `33323759087` for sysinfoviewer PR #1849, head `6c358fc988d82e667fbdae80e8ddf0eda22c03df`, stopped in dependency preparation with exit code 124 after downloading part of a 418-package closure; the log ended with `context canceled`. Its classifier instead selected `needs-native-riscv` solely because the ordinary DNF package list contained `kmod-libs`. The acceptance target is that an explicit capped timeout is classified as infrastructure and that an installed package name alone cannot assert a native-hardware requirement.
- **Environment, cleanup, and inputs:** the isolated infrastructure worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-ci-timeout-classifier.lvNKSq/worktree` started clean from protected `main` `dc29f3c757e87a63408d4db30c7c0aca980e16d4` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and 44 GiB free disk, with Git 2.50.1, GitHub CLI 2.97.0, and Python 3.9.6. The exact #1849 artifact is isolated under `/private/tmp/pr1849-run33323759087.BMYpyM`. `make clean` removed validation outputs before the evidence round. No local RPM, QEMU, Docker, target build, publication, or native-RISC-V test ran.
- **Implementation and validation:** the native rule no longer treats the bare package/tool family name `kmod` as proof; concrete kernel-module operations and paths such as `modprobe`, `insmod`, and `/lib/modules` remain native signals. Structured nonzero `exit_code` is now admitted as failure evidence, and exit 124 or `context canceled` maps to infrastructure. A regression fixture reproduces the exact 418-package/`kmod-libs`/timeout shape and requires `failure:infrastructure` without a native label. Fresh `make validate`, `make test` (124/124), `make golden`, the focused six-test classifier suite, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Protected dispatch and Runner anomaly:** protected-main dispatch `33347777626` for #1849 and `33347886353` for snorenotify #1833 both passed authorization, exact package overlay, scope, metadata, source, repository, patch, and merge-policy gates, then failed before checkout or package execution on runners `oe-rva23-qemu-238` and `oe-rva23-qemu-242`. Each pre-job hook correctly refused mutation after detecting a running container. Strict SSH identity was established from isolated scanned keys only after both ED25519 fingerprints exactly matched the previously recorded fleet fingerprint `SHA256:lHG3sACIuiIMoJH/nta2A1amKGy0d3UA7GXeOr68/qk`. Read-only inspection found one repository-derived `openeuler-builddeps-*` container running for six days on each host, with the pinned openEuler 24.03 LTS SP3 RVA23 image; no container has yet been stopped or deleted. A #1835 dispatch attempt failed closed on a transient GitHub status API EOF before a workflow was created.
- **Truthful boundary and next action:** these protected runs provide no package-build, RPM, smoke, or publication evidence. Audit all 50 hosts with pinned identity and exact container metadata while GitHub reports the fleet idle; remove only independently proven orphaned repository build containers through a separately recorded bounded cleanup, then retry one protected canary before broad dispatch. In parallel, submit exact-head package-local repairs already supported by complete artifacts. No RPM or SRPM URL is claimed.

Ubuntu systemd 259 returns nonzero for the repository's verb-first `systemctl is-active --quiet` and `is-enabled --quiet` form, so the preserved audit JSON falsely reports both service fields as false even though independent `systemctl is-active UNIT` and `is-enabled UNIT` checks, preflight, process state, and GitHub state all pass. Cleanup receives the image reference from the existing digest lock. The systemd calls in install-time activation, registration, and audit now place global `--quiet` before the verb for systemd 259 compatibility; regression coverage binds all three scripts and both safe-recovery paths. The existing installer already deploys `audit.sh`, so the observed installed-file drift remains a fleet redeployment item rather than an unrecorded remote modification in this recovery batch.
- **Validation, evidence boundary, and next action:** after `make clean`, fresh `make validate`, `make test` (128/128), `make golden`, `make dashboard`, the focused Runner/build-user suite (43/43), and `git diff --check` all passed. No local RPM, QEMU, target Docker build, package publication, or supplemental-repository upload ran. Deleted containers themselves are not recoverable, but their locked base image and all non-container Docker state were preserved and normal CI recreates the ephemeral object. Submit this prevention change as a separate infrastructure PR, enable guarded squash auto-merge, then run exactly one protected-main package canary. Resume bounded parallel timeout retries only if that canary reaches package work; continue to withhold RPM/SRPM URLs without matching immutable publication evidence.

## 2026-08-31 — Codex — Ubuntu 26.04 Docker iptables deployment contract

- **Goal, exact failure, and hypothesis:** diagnose protected canary run `33350701571` on Runner `oe-rva23-qemu-227` without changing that host. The run is bound to protected-main SHA `13e152c8e7a4f9456bea416376f62857e9801b26`; it passed authorization, overlay, scope, metadata, source, patch, and repository gates, then Docker 29 failed while setup-qemu created a bridge endpoint because its configured `iptables` firewall backend could not execute `/usr/sbin/iptables`. This is Runner package/deployment drift, not package-source or RISC-V build evidence.
- **Environment, cleanup, and inputs:** local work used isolated branch/worktree `infra/runner-iptables-ubuntu2604-20260831023043` at `/private/tmp/runner-iptables-local-fix-zRa2e8/worktree` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 43 GiB free disk. Fresh run JSON/logs and official Ubuntu package metadata are retained under `/private/tmp/runner-iptables-local-fix-zRa2e8`; exact canary artifacts are under `/private/tmp/pr1849-protected-canary-after-runner-recovery.7h9pQL`. The evidence-producing validation round starts with `make clean`. No local RPM/QEMU/Docker target build, publication, or supplemental-repository upload ran.
- **Fleet evidence and bounded canary repair:** a strict-host-key, read-only 50-host probe in `/private/tmp/runner-fleet-iptables-readonly.60g5xx/results.tsv`, SHA-256 `24a321ace29460838c15bb08884db2f28d98d81e7d84e853cc07c74e9f73bb31`, found `/usr/sbin/iptables` absent on all 50 otherwise empty Docker daemons, while every alternatives database selected `/usr/sbin/iptables-nft`. On idle `.227`, `dpkg` independently confirmed `iptables` `1.8.11-2ubuntu3` was already installed and its selected target existed; only the master link was absent. After API-idle, exact-unit stop, API-offline, zero Worker/Listener, and empty-Docker gates, `update-alternatives --auto iptables` explicitly reported that it was repairing a broken link group. A no-pull bridge-container smoke then printed binfmt `qemu/v9.2.0`, auto-removed its only container, and the Runner returned active/enabled, preflight-passed, API-online/idle. Evidence `/private/tmp/runner227-iptables-canary-repair.O4NrFd` binds the before, repair, smoke, and final states. No image, volume, network, package, workspace, or unrelated container was deleted.
- **Package-contract evidence and implementation:** Ubuntu 26.04 `docker.io` `29.1.3-0ubuntu4.1` declares `iptables` as a dependency, and Ubuntu `iptables` `1.8.11-2ubuntu3` supplies nft/legacy frontends selected through alternatives at `/usr/sbin/iptables`. The fleet installer now names `iptables` directly in its minimal `--no-install-recommends` package contract, runs `update-alternatives --auto iptables` so an already-installed but broken master link is repaired, and requires the exact alternatives link and executable to work. Service preflight repeats the executable check before a Runner can accept work. No broader firewall-policy change was added.
- **Deployment-drift boundary:** a reviewed fleet redeployment is defined as rerunning the complete installer from one exact protected-main archive, not copying individual helpers. For each API-online/idle host, stop only the exact Runner unit, require API offline, zero Worker processes, and no running containers, preserve the immutable installed identity including its actual `ALLOW_DEGRADED` value, rerun `install.sh`, then activate the reviewed policy. Advance only after installed preflight and audit, service active/enabled, API online/idle, and empty Docker inventory all pass. This supported path refreshes the missing installed `audit.sh` and stale cleanup helpers together; any identity, package, cleanup, audit, or state mismatch stops rollout.
- **Acceptance boundary and next action:** after `make clean`, the amended code passed 25 focused Runner tests, error-level ShellCheck, `make validate`, the 129-test full suite, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, and `git diff --check`. The `.227` smoke proves only the Docker bridge prerequisite, not a package build. Submit this as a separate infrastructure PR; after protected merge, redeploy and audit `.227` from the complete exact archive, rerun one protected package canary, and expand only after that run reaches package work. No RPM or SRPM was produced and no publication URL is claimed.

## 2026-08-31 — Codex — fleet-wide systemctl compromise quarantine and fail-closed gate

- **Goal, environment, and acceptance target:** convert the observed Runner lifecycle anomalies into a package-integrity gate without treating a renamed executable or successful unit transition as host health. Local implementation uses isolated branch/worktree `infra/runner-systemd-integrity-20260831032638` at `/private/tmp/runner-systemd-integrity-fix.6B9z8w/worktree`, based on protected `main` `011eeba0be2699edf26f21ec758f259a4bf366cb`, on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 42 GiB free disk. The gate must reject any altered Ubuntu `systemd` package before the first systemctl call, including the observed case where dpkg verification prints a checksum mismatch but returns zero. No local RPM, QEMU, Docker target build, publication, or supplemental-repository upload runs.
- **Fifty-host IOC evidence:** the strict-host-key read-only fleet scan `/private/tmp/runner-fleet-systemctl-integrity-readonly2.F5aYsc/summary.tsv`, SHA-256 `b077e0d72614997bd5ca1a71f66f54d20328850dda5294a3cf96d9687628a245`, contains 50/50 matching rows. Every host had substituted `/usr/bin/systemctl` MD5 `d5462e5598d0598ed181b0e9d38ac9bc` instead of package MD5 `1de2a43ad3f79154aaac4ab01b363c8b`; an unowned renamed file retained the package MD5; and `dpkg --verify systemd` reported `??5?????? /usr/bin/systemctl`. On `.227`, bounded evidence `/private/tmp/runner-227-systemctl-readonly.Zh5DTY` additionally binds the replacement time to an enabled unowned root service and journal deletion. This is fleet compromise evidence, not a systemd 259 return-code defect or proof that a host is safe because a unit transition completed.
- **Reversible scheduling quarantine:** the separately authorized response evidence `/private/tmp/runner-fleet-security-quarantine-final.1OiNWR` records all 50 exact Runner units disabled, no target Listener or Worker process, no container, and GitHub 50/50 offline with zero busy. `final-summary.tsv` SHA-256 is `e611aecdb09fe74264213fe0cf60d1c3fb0dac79a9b3210e12c857dc4cb7cbd9`; `api-final.json` SHA-256 is `70f9cf2cf07feaa5ffa7ac2e6f9f9c0b19eefe7ebf21d355ffd1beb77434dbce`. This is reversible scheduling isolation only: it neither removes persistence nor establishes host cleanliness. The hosts remain quarantined pending forensic preservation, SSH/Runner credential rotation, and trusted reimaging.
- **Implementation and security boundary:** `_lib.sh` now requires `/usr/bin/systemctl` to be a root-owned, mode-0755, non-symlink regular executable; binds exact dpkg owner, installed package name/status/version; and requires empty stdout from `dpkg-query --verify systemd`, independently of its return code. The shared systemctl wrapper performs that gate before every absolute-path, no-pager invocation. `oe_assert_platform` gates its first system state read, the installer rechecks immediately after apt, and preflight/audit plus all lifecycle scripts inherit the same behavior. No indicator-specific executable is invoked, restored, deleted, or accepted as a repair.
- **Validation and next action:** after `make clean`, the focused Runner suite passed 26/26 including the rc-zero/nonempty-verification regression; error-level ShellCheck, `make validate`, the complete 138-test suite, `make golden`, and `git diff --check` all passed. Authenticated local credential guard passed without recording a token immediately before the local commit. Submit the result as a separate infrastructure PR, but do not reactivate the quarantined fleet after merge. Reimage and rotate credentials through a separately reviewed incident-response milestone, then readmit one clean canary only after package integrity, Docker/QEMU/preflight/audit, API idle, and empty-container gates all pass.

## 2026-08-31 — Codex — protected-base CI tooling with exact package overlays

- **Goal, hypothesis, and acceptance target:** repair the CI compatibility failure exposed by PR #349 run `33359635910` without executing a stale or untrusted PR copy of shared CI tooling. The hypothesis was that the package workflow checked out the exact PR head in every job, so the current workflow passed `--allow-unavailable` to the older PR-head `ci/rpm-repo-client.py`. Acceptance required all seven package-processing jobs to check out immutable protected base/main tooling, overlay only the exact `packages/<id>` tree, bind package commit, package id, package tree SHA, and tooling SHA through existing fail-closed evidence, retain GitHub-hosted routing for pull requests and merge groups, and leave all remote state untouched.
- **Environment, repository, and cleanup:** work ran on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, approximately 41 GiB free disk, Git 2.50.1, Python 3.9.6, GNU Make 3.81, and actionlint from `/opt/homebrew/bin/actionlint`. A fresh fetch resolved `origin/main` exactly to `c2b7b2f3980736abee1c24c2b329a75cdce36b32`. The clean isolated worktree is `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr349-infra-overlay.SBrguY/worktree`; the environment record is `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr349-infra-overlay.SBrguY/environment.txt`. Validation outputs were isolated under `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr349-infra-overlay.SBrguY/validation`, and the evidence-producing gate starts with `make clean`. No RPM, QEMU, Docker target build, Runner connection, publication, push, label, merge, or CI trigger ran.
- **Exact failure evidence and interpretation:** `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr349-wdiff-repair.KFhwqt/remote-submit/new-run-33359635910-final-snapshot.json` binds run `33359635910` to PR #349 head `b38530e76e6bcd12cedea49e3b90f6f1e1fdc14c` and protected base `e0ff52a84ba002c91749e7744432d17aaeaebafa`. The preserved failed-job log records `rpm-repo-client.py: error: unrecognized arguments: --allow-unavailable`; the later build and smoke errors only report the missing repository artifact. The first real error is therefore a stale shared-helper/workflow compatibility failure, not a wdiff package, dependency, native-RISC-V, or publication failure.
- **Implementation and security contract:** `.github/workflows/package-ci.yml` now checks out `${pull_request.base.sha || merge_group.base_sha || github.sha}` in prepare, metadata, patch, source, repository, RPM build, and smoke. Protected `ci/detect-change-scope.py` first identifies a single-package delta; protected `ci/materialize-package-head.py` then restores only that exact package tree and records the package commit, package tree SHA, and protected tooling commit. Protected `ci/select-package-scope.py` revalidates those identities plus the index/worktree/untracked-file state before package policy or build processing begins. Infrastructure changes retain the protected-base checkout and never overlay PR tooling. The two heavy-job runner expressions remain restricted to protected-main `push` or `workflow_dispatch`; pull requests and merge groups remain on GitHub-hosted VMs. Repository validation and focused tests now reject reintroduction of a PR-head shared-tool checkout or a missing provenance-bound overlay.
- **Validation, limitations, and next action:** after `make clean`, `make validate`, the complete 138-test suite, `make golden`, the focused materializer/selector/scope suite (16/16), actionlint, `git diff --check`, and authenticated local credential guard all passed; logs are under `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr349-infra-overlay.SBrguY/validation`. A fresh detached integration worktree then classified exact PR #349 head `b38530e7` against current protected tooling `c2b7b2f3` as the single `wdiff` package, overlaid package tree `1a59992861605b02d38354563daa08359b2ca24c`, revalidated every identity, and confirmed that the retained protected `rpm-repo-client.py` supports `--allow-unavailable`; its evidence is `exact-pr-overlay-integration.log`. Pull-request infrastructure changes cannot prove their new shared-helper behavior by executing that helper from the untrusted head under this contract; local tests/actionlint are therefore the pre-merge evidence, followed by one protected-main post-merge canary. The separate state-contract gap remains recorded but out of this patch's scope: the classifier can emit `failure:infrastructure`, while `.github/labels.json` does not yet register it and `ci/label-ci-result.sh` does not synchronize `failure:*`. Address that in a separate reviewed infrastructure change so this trust-boundary repair stays minimal. Retain only a local commit for review; do not submit it until the parent milestone explicitly authorizes a remote PR.

- **Ski IA-64 compressed-manual repair:** exact run `33362442642` at head `d8eef81a0a6b711060267a6d424b28eab9378b68` installed dependencies, compiled the patched simulator, and passed all upstream tests. RPM then failed only because `%install` generated a file list before `brp-compress` renamed `ski.1`, `bski.1`, and `bskinc.1`. Release 4 excludes the manual tree from the pre-compression generated list and owns it with `%{_mandir}/man1/*`. Artifact `9748726205` was downloaded into `/private/tmp/ski-pr1868-run33362442642.Kyj59T`, and its ZIP SHA-256 `dcb75844b1b119e0d9bd1b393d90bdae423be39573a92d11573de245d2a1dbd6` matched the Actions API digest. Libpsl #184 failed because the shared dispatcher invoked a helper absent from the old exact package head, before dependency or target work. Entr #224 reached rpmbuild on two exact runs, but its internal build artifact expired and now returns HTTP 410, so the first real compiler/test error cannot be recovered and no speculative repair was made. The complete audit roots are `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr203-libtool-exact.vdHyP4`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr172-pixman-exact.EJkQPd`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr139-libunistring-audit.q5xH5y`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr184-libpsl-audit.SwJSRN`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr224-exact.45QEJc`, and `/private/tmp/pr182-checksec-audit.5vdSS0`.
