## 2026-09-02 — Codex — propagate validated rpmbuild timeout through the container wrapper

- **Goal and hypothesis:** close the infrastructure defect exposed by GEOS #285 exact-head run `33543646431` at `fc29e324e38a1fcdc2209c0c53050a3b2ab32a2d`. The run requested a 180-minute package budget and recorded 9,981 seconds remaining after dependency preparation, but `ci/run-rpmbuild-container.py` omitted `scripts/build-rpm --timeout`; the inner script therefore stopped at its 7,200-second default while normal compilation was at 51%. The hypothesis was that explicitly carrying the already validated positive remaining value through both wrapper layers would remove that unintended cap without changing runner authority, package behavior, or test coverage.
- **Environment and isolation:** work ran on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB memory, and approximately 41 GiB free disk. A fresh worktree `/private/tmp/geos-timeout-propagation-fix.eWPM3a/worktree` and branch `infra/propagate-rpmbuild-timeout-20260902130120` were created from fetched `origin/main` `572c0e3cabd97140ea4c493781a9bb842d470920`; the pre-existing dirty user checkout was not modified. `make clean` removed generated validation state before both focused and full evidence-producing runs.
- **Implementation:** `.github/workflows/package-ci.yml` now passes `rpmbuild_timeout_seconds`, emitted by the existing deadline helper, to the trusted container orchestrator as `--build-timeout-seconds`. `ci/run-rpmbuild-container.py` accepts only canonical positive decimal values, rejects invalid direct-call values fail-closed, and adds the same value to the protected inner `scripts/build-rpm --timeout` command. The outer `run-capped` process receives exactly 60 additional seconds so the inner script can terminate rpmbuild and persist `rpmbuild-phase-result.json` before the container is killed; for the observed 9,981-second remaining budget, inner and outer limits are therefore 9,981 and 10,041 seconds. This 60-second grace borrows from, but does not extend, the existing 300-second evidence reserve, leaving at least 240 seconds for classification and upload. Both values are checked as positive decimals before use. Repository static validation parses the reserve and grace, rejects the former same-deadline race, requires the two distinct propagation paths, and enforces the 240-second remainder. Focused regression coverage proves the 9,981-to-10,041 relationship and rejects zero, negative, signed, fractional, textual, boolean, and wrong-type inputs. No package, source, test selection, runner label, permission, native-routing policy, dependency timeout, or publication behavior changed.
- **Commands and results:** `make clean`, `make validate`, `make test`, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, `git diff --check`, Python compilation, and `python3 -m unittest -v scripts.tests.test_ci_build_user scripts.tests.test_rpmbuild_timeout_budget` all passed, then all gates were repeated after adding the reviewed outer/inner evidence grace. The final complete suite passed 146 tests; the final focused timeout/build-user suite passed 27 tests. Initial logs are under `/private/tmp/geos-timeout-propagation-fix.eWPM3a/validation`; authoritative post-review logs and environment records are under `/private/tmp/geos-timeout-propagation-fix.eWPM3a/validation-race-fix`. The originating exact-run audit, including eight API-matched artifact digests and Source0 verification, is `/private/tmp/pr285-geos-fresh-ci-audit.H8Mflr`.
- **Boundary and next action:** no local RPM, QEMU, target Docker build, fleet access, push, PR, rerun, publication, or other remote mutation occurred. This static repair does not prove GEOS will finish inside its true remaining 9,981-second budget and creates no RPM/SRPM or publication evidence. After independent review, commit the infrastructure-only diff, submit it through protected GitHub-hosted checks, and rerun GEOS unchanged only after the fix reaches protected main.

## 2026-09-02 — Codex — restore the immutable image's live RPM baseline

- **Goal, hypothesis, and environment:** address the repeated 3,600-second pre-rpmbuild dependency failures without borrowing time from the validated package build budget. Exact artifacts from Diamond, Vncmanager, Xapp, and Shmux all used locked image `sha256:c9d8e675e04cd0ef61c5b2e77d530652db79de51431dc047277cdbd1f63b32b3` but recorded an empty `rpm_manifest_before`, then installed 125, 176, 427, and 65 packages respectively. The hypothesis is an RPM database-path mismatch between the Fedora bootstrap implementation and the openEuler target implementation; the implementation deliberately discovers both paths with their own `rpm --eval '%{_dbpath}'` rather than guessing `/var/lib/rpm`, `/usr/lib/sysimage/rpm`, or a symlink. Work used fresh branch `infra/trusted-rpm-baseline-20260902133114` and worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-trusted-rpm-baseline.W2fOFP/worktree` from fetched protected `main` `57d5ece19cbf4ccfe562e6d2da9a8446960bb83a` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 40 GiB free disk.
- **Implementation and fail-closed boundary:** one shared query-format helper now creates both bootstrap and target sorted RPM manifests. The bootstrap records the database path evaluated by its own RPM. In the target-platform image stage, target RPM independently evaluates its runtime database path; a dedicated finalizer validates both absolute paths, copies the bootstrap database only when the evaluated paths differ and the target path is absent or empty, never creates a symlink, and requires target RPM to read a non-empty result. `verify-target.sh` then requires the live manifest to contain bash, rpm, rpm-build, gcc, gcc-c++, make, and python3 and to exactly equal the checksum-bound embedded bootstrap manifest. CI image evidence retains a second live manifest and compares it again. Dependency preparation creates its container with no network, atomically records structured `dependency-rpm-baseline` evidence with `network_install_started: false`, and refuses network connection or DNF installation when the live manifest is empty, malformed, or missing an anchor; the resulting failure class is infrastructure and cannot authorize a package source patch. Only after `docker network connect` returns successfully does it atomically advance the same evidence to `network_install_started: true` / `connected-before-install`, and that durable update must succeed before any DNF transaction begins; a failed connection therefore cannot claim networking started.
- **Cleanup, validation, and evidence:** authoritative generated validation used isolated `/private/tmp/openeuler-trusted-rpm-baseline.W2fOFP/validation-state-correction/evidence`, beginning with `make clean`. `make validate`, all 158 repository tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, all 72 focused baseline/timeout/repository tests, Bash syntax, warning-level ShellCheck, Actionlint, Python compilation, credential guard, and `git diff --check` passed after the network-state evidence correction. The preceding read-only design evidence and four runtime-manifest observations are under `/private/tmp/openeuler-dependency-budget-audit.jnQLdK`; local implementation evidence remains under `/private/tmp/openeuler-trusted-rpm-baseline.W2fOFP`. No local RPM, target Docker/QEMU build, image publication, image-lock edit, repository-server action, remote GitHub mutation, or quarantined fleet access occurred.
- **Limitation and next action:** local static checks cannot prove the two runtime database paths or their database-format compatibility. The infrastructure PR path is sufficient for the required proof: `build-ci-image.yml` is now triggered by both new helpers, builds on a disposable GitHub-hosted runner, executes the finalizer and exact manifest comparison under target RISC-V QEMU, uploads the evaluated evidence, and does not publish on a pull request. Merge only after that image job proves a non-empty exact live manifest. A protected-main push will then publish a new digest and create the separate `ci/image.lock` PR. Until that lock merges, the new dependency preflight intentionally rejects the old empty-baseline image rather than redownloading an entire rootfs; package runs during that interval are expected to fail closed as infrastructure.

## 2026-09-02 — Codex — fail-closed single-package Auto-merge scope

- **Goal, hypothesis, environment, and isolation:** explain why infrastructure PRs could merge while their relevant CI failed, then ensure repository automation never arms GitHub Auto-merge for a non-single-package change. Work used fresh branch `fix/auto-merge-fail-closed-577a19232e56` and worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-auto-merge-safety.D2wj18/worktree` from exact protected `main` `577a19232e56358e9f940bed56ca7c5af341703d` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 40 GiB free disk. The pre-existing checkouts and user changes were not modified. Fresh generated validation began with `make clean`; validation logs are under `/private/tmp/openeuler-auto-merge-safety.D2wj18/validation-logs.GvSGtM`.
- **Observed root cause:** ruleset `20579949` requires only `metadata-validate`, `source-verify`, `rpmbuild-riscv64`, `rpm-install-smoke`, `patch-policy`, and `merge-policy`, while the former Auto Merge Policy armed every trusted same-repository non-draft PR without checking its files. PR #1996 changed 12 workflow/CI/script/tracking files; all six required Package CI contexts were `success`, but Build CI Image and Golden Evaluation failed, so the unrequired failures did not block the already armed squash merge. PR #1997 changed six workflow/CI/test/tracking files; trusted dispatch failed and the six downstream required contexts were all `skipped`, which GitHub treated as satisfying the rule, so it also merged. Read-only API evidence is retained in `/private/tmp/openeuler-auto-merge-safety.D2wj18/pr-1996.json`, `pr-1996-files.json`, `pr-1996-check-runs.json`, and the corresponding `pr-1997-*` files.
- **Implementation and safety contract:** `.github/workflows/auto-merge.yml` now disarms any existing request before checkout or evaluation and proves through API readback that Auto-merge is off. It re-evaluates `converted_to_draft` and `edited` in addition to open, synchronization, readiness, and label transitions, so a previously armed PR is disarmed when its draft or edited metadata/base state changes rather than relying on implicit GitHub behavior. It checks out only the event's immutable protected-base SHA, downloads the complete paginated current PR file list, and runs new `ci/evaluate-auto-merge.py`. Eligibility requires an open, non-draft, trusted, same-repository PR with unchanged full head/base leases, no blocking label, a complete file count, canonical paths, and exactly one `packages/<package-id>/` directory. Workflow, CI, scripts, schemas, catalog, Dashboard, documentation, mixed packages, duplicate/incomplete responses, and renames from shared paths are blocked. Only `eligible=true` can reach the arming step; a post-arm lease/readback failure triggers an EXIT rollback and must prove Auto-merge is off. `ci/validate-repository.py` makes the event coverage, ordering, protected-base checkout, complete pagination, policy helper, and rollback contract repository invariants. README now defines the scope before relying on it.
- **Regression coverage and validation:** `scripts/tests/test_auto_merge_policy.py` covers ordinary and paginated single-package success, every shared path class, two-package changes, incomplete pagination, renamed infrastructure, draft/label/head drift, explicit `converted_to_draft`/`edited` workflow coverage, the exact #1996 all-six-success shape, the exact #1997 all-six-skipped shape, and workflow disarm/evaluate/arm/rollback ordering. Python compilation, focused 10/10 tests, Actionlint over every workflow, `make validate`, the final complete 176-test suite, `make golden`, and `make dashboard` passed. After the independent event-coverage review, focused 10/10 tests, Actionlint, and `make validate` were rerun against the final trigger list. The first combined command reached the tool's 30-second response boundary after full tests and golden output; Dashboard generation was rerun independently and produced 151,852 inventory rows and 403 package records. An earlier attempt to replace a log directory was rejected by the execution safety policy before any test ran, so a new unique log directory was used instead.
- **First-deployment bootstrap:** Auto Merge Policy run `33643701389` on infrastructure PR #2000 proved the mandatory disarm and immutable protected-base checkout, then failed with exit 127 because exact base `95a8bba2` necessarily predates `ci/evaluate-auto-merge.py`. The workflow now treats only that missing/non-executable protected-base evaluator as a successful blocked bootstrap: it writes the normal policy fields with `status=blocked`, `eligible=false`, empty `package_id`, exact event head/base, no changed-file claim, and reason `protected-base policy predates evaluator`; it appends only false/empty outputs and exits before any files API evaluation or arming step. It never checks out, imports, or executes PR-head policy code. Static and unit tests lock disarm-before-checkout, protected-base-checkout-before-fallback, fallback-before-evaluator, and fallback-before-arm ordering. After the fix reaches protected main, a canary must exercise the real base evaluator; bootstrap-blocked evidence does not prove a single-package PR can be armed.
- **Boundary and next action:** no local RPM, QEMU, target Docker build, runner-fleet access, package publication, commit, push, PR, label, merge, rerun, or other remote mutation occurred. This is static policy evidence, not proof of a live GitHub transition. Publish the eventual infrastructure PR as draft with `needs-human` under the old policy, keep its Auto-merge disabled, and merge only after independent review and protected GitHub-hosted checks. After the fix reaches main, exercise one single-package PR and one draft infrastructure canary; acceptance requires the package PR alone to become armed and the infrastructure PR to remain unarmed. Update this record with those exact run/head/API readbacks.

## 2026-09-02 — Codex — rebuild the target RPM database through RPM's portable header stream

- **Goal and decisive evidence:** replace the incompatible raw database copy without guessing either distribution's database path and without inventing installed-package records. GitHub-hosted PR #1998 `Build CI Image` run `33641686343`, job `100286072008`, is exact-head terminal failure at `5f26bb2a787b254b2d8b120a0a304aaa287f363c`; API head, fetched pull ref, and run head agree. Its diagnostic log proves Fedora bootstrap RPM 4.20.1 used `/usr/lib/sysimage/rpm` and created a 9,965,568-byte SQLite database, while openEuler target RPM 4.18.2 evaluates `/var/lib/rpm`. Target queries against both a fresh copy and the relocated default database returned status zero but zero package records. The only live artifact, ID `9851192247`, has matching API and measured ZIP SHA-256 `2a83163312b9f49cd0b188b562760c7f5deeb330334b840f98126970e6a79d0d`; it is build evidence, not RPM/SRPM or publication evidence. Audit files are `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmdb-rebuild-run33641686343.rlZ333`.
- **Environment and official contract:** work uses fresh branch `infra/rpmdb-target-rebuild-20260902223107` and worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmdb-target-rebuild.CH8DKS/worktree` from fetched protected `main` `577a19232e56358e9f940bed56ca7c5af341703d` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 38 GiB free disk. RPM upstream's exact 4.18.2 `rpmdb(8)` documentation states that `--exportdb` emits a header-list format suitable for transport to another host or database type and `--importdb` imports that format. The source document and RPM/DNF primary documentation snapshots are retained under the audit directory.
- **Implementation and trust boundary:** after the existing gpgchecked, dependency-resolved bootstrap DNF transaction, bootstrap RPM first verifies its database, exports the complete installed package headers, checksum-binds the stream, imports it into a clean round-trip database, verifies that database, and requires both the full exported header stream and sorted package identity manifest to reproduce exactly under the same RPM implementation. RPM 4.18.2's primary source implements export as `headerWrite(..., HEADER_MAGIC_YES)` over every installed header and import as `headerRead(..., HEADER_MAGIC_YES)` plus `rpmtsImportHeader`. The initial implementation attempted a deliberately strict byte-for-byte cross-version re-export gate; the live result and correction below supersede that unsupported assumption. In target stage, target RPM 4.18.2 checksum-verifies and imports the stream into a clean, non-overlapping staging database selected without a distribution path constant. It verifies the staging database and exact-compares the package identity and immutable-header-digest manifest. The evaluated target runtime path must be absent or empty; an unexpected record or file fails closed and is never deleted. Once staging is completely verified, an equal-device check, removal of only an empty runtime directory, and atomic rename place the target-generated database before manifest and low-level verification repeat. The former bootstrap database is removed only after target verification succeeds. No `--initdb`, `--justdb`, dependency bypass, signature bypass, package cache replay, empty database shortcut, hard-coded `/var/lib/rpm` or `/usr/lib/sysimage/rpm`, or second synthetic install transaction is used; imported records derive only from the original trusted DNF transaction.
- **Boundary and next action:** local static work cannot execute either target RPM or QEMU. GitHub-hosted Build CI Image must prove RPM 4.20.1 export and RPM 4.18.2 import interoperability, exact live immutable-header-digest manifest equality, and `verifydb` success before merge. PR validation must remain non-publishing; no `ci/image.lock` update is part of this change.
- **Commands and results:** validation began with `make clean` in isolated `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmdb-target-rebuild.CH8DKS/validation/evidence`. `make validate`, all 168 repository tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, all 69 focused baseline/classifier/build-user/repository tests, Bash syntax, warning-level ShellCheck, Actionlint, Python compilation, `git diff --check`, and the local credential guard passed. No local target image, RPM, QEMU, or target-container build was executed.
- **First live interoperability result and corrected gate:** draft PR #2001 exact head `b089a56baa2fd7c6d6337a3484255a1778966fd8` ran Build CI Image `33645019773` on GitHub host `runnervmejwal` (`10.1.0.12`, Ubuntu kernel `6.17.0-1022-azure`, 4 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, about 88 GiB free disk, Docker 28.0.4). The gpgchecked bootstrap transaction, portable-stream checksum, target 4.18.2 import, `verifydb`, and exact package identity manifest comparison all passed. The run failed only when the target 4.18.2 re-exported stream was byte-compared with the bootstrap 4.20.1 stream. RPM documents header-list transport across hosts or database types but does not define cross-version re-export bytes as canonical. The strict manifest is therefore extended with `SHA1HEADER` and `SHA256HEADER`, which RPM 4.18.2 defines as the SHA-1 and SHA-256 digests of the package header, and remains exact across bootstrap staging, target staging, target runtime, and post-build probes. The unsupported cross-version raw-byte equality gate is removed; the original stream remains checksum-bound, the same-version bootstrap round trip remains byte-exact, and target `verifydb` remains mandatory. Artifact ID `9852507488` has API digest `sha256:c86e48ee5f70bcf4dbaaf2f03dc3b9970a5156ab6a55d559e54a9aaf31a90485`; downloaded evidence is `/private/tmp/pr2001-artifact.yUsD1p`, and failure logs are `/private/tmp/pr2001-failure-audit.bCQtie`. This run published nothing.
- **Concurrent baseline and package observations:** Golden Evaluation `33645019980` used locked image `sha256:c9d8e675e04cd0ef61c5b2e77d530652db79de51431dc047277cdbd1f63b32b3`; its `rpm-baseline.json` reports zero live records and all required anchors missing, so the failure is an old-image infrastructure result rather than a golden-package regression. PR #1060 exact head `e3cf6b0a2d785b3ba24c6ecb9c8fed673abf41d6` likewise stopped before rpmbuild on the empty locked-image baseline and produced no RPM, SRPM, or publication evidence. GEOS #285 run `33634913667` at `f4aac3f8ba2f1e5db1a2f57759466554521fe0a0` and diamond #1922 run `33634962291` at `391d242d385814e73b3c3196f37f57f62ebed977` remain in their 180-minute build windows with no terminal verdict; audit evidence is `/private/tmp/openeuler-geos-diamond-current-audit.vvI0Pr`.
- **Successful semantic-header canary and format hardening:** exact head `40d7c12dbfcf31dcd7ef0d218183633f4c46e76c` passed Build CI Image run `33646413132`: target import and `verifydb`, the 278-row embedded/live header-digest manifest, and the RVA23 Zba/Zbb/Zbs probe all passed. Artifact `9853101634` has matching API and measured ZIP digest `sha256:eecc83d68e3a759d4f1521cc0e16bf01bb2e4bda6110be790c9d94ff0c37cbee`; evidence is `/private/tmp/pr2001-success-artifact.gvZHeO` and `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr2001-workflows.k8tRc1`. All 278 SHA-1/SHA-256 header fields were valid 40/64-character lowercase hexadecimal values. The legacy `SIGPGP` query field was `(none)` for all 278 records and is therefore removed rather than misrepresented as signature evidence; transaction trust remains the original gpgchecked DNF install. ## 2026-09-03 — Codex — close the Auto-merge immediate-completion race

- **Goal, environment, and isolation:** make the single-package Auto-merge transaction fail closed when all pre-existing package checks are already successful, without changing any package. No commit, push, PR, label, ruleset write, workflow rerun, package build, publication, runner login, or fleet recovery was performed.
- **Observed race and exact evidence:** Auto Merge Policy run `33669948581` at package head `1805c5d0670683252419048fa171d287d829b89d` disarmed and evaluated PR #1060 successfully, then invoked `gh pr merge --auto` after all six existing required checks had already passed. GitHub immediately merged the PR at `2026-09-02T18:53:20Z` as protected-main commit `14e4d985378c05cd4cccde4748ec9a98408452b9`; the arming step subsequently failed at `18:53:23Z` because its post-arm readback expected a non-null Auto-merge request. The EXIT rollback then saw the merged PR's `auto_merge=null` and incorrectly accepted that field alone as proof of disarm even though state was `closed`, `merged=true`, and `merged_at` was non-null. Read-only run JSON and failed log are `/private/tmp/automerge-race-fix.pqVAba/run-33669948581.json` and `run-33669948581-failed.log`, SHA-256 `dcd2b4ea7e55d327dbf68a8b3093d847535588e04b81c8a0d8ec46d5ff76d644` and `3170be6759484e4c6fb4d6412844ab829dc7818edc99f97b7fd8cee140f8e239`.
- **Implementation and state contract:** `.github/rulesets/main.json` requires the established Auto Merge Policy `configure` context after the six package contexts. That check stays pending while its own disarm/evaluate/arm/readback transaction runs, preventing an already-green PR from completing during `gh pr merge --auto`; only the successful job conclusion can release the merge. Reusing the established context avoids renaming the check on already-open exact heads. Mandatory-disarm, pre-arm, post-arm, and rollback readbacks bind repository, exact event head/base, `state=open`, `merged=false`, and `merged_at=null`, as well as the expected Auto-merge field. An already merged, closed, changed-lease, or cross-repository PR can no longer satisfy rollback proof. `ci/configure-github.sh` reads back the applied ruleset by exact ID, projects out only API-stable policy fields, and exact-compares them with the committed configuration. A readback failure or mismatch attempts to restore the exact preceding policy and verifies that restoration; an unverifiable rollback is reported as an urgent unknown live state. Repository validation and README are synchronized with the context and accurately distinguish blocking automatic merge from a separately authorized manual maintainer merge.
- **Validation and anomaly:** after the first clean validation correctly exposed one stale static marker that still expected the replaced pre-arm shell assertion, the validator was updated to require the new state predicates and the complete clean round passed: metadata, 151,852-entry package index, repository validation, all 190 unit tests, Golden materialization, and Dashboard generation. The final focused Auto-merge suite passed 13/13; Actionlint over all workflows, warning-level ShellCheck, Bash/Python/JSON syntax, configuration dry-run, `git diff --check`, and authenticated local credential guard also passed. Dry-run evidence is `/private/tmp/automerge-race-fix.pqVAba/configure-dry-run-final.json`, SHA-256 `5c3909babe90192ee619256dad032863765d23e186006d1189a1612c5743f426`. ## 2026-09-03 — Codex — gate the required-context migration before any write

- **Goal and implementation:** turn the one-time `configure` coverage study into a reusable fail-closed gate. The live activation helper now exact-compares the protected branch conditions, requires empty exclusions and bypass actors, and rejects drift. Create responses with invalid JSON or scalar shapes enter discovery rollback, mutated exact readbacks with invalid identity or policy shape enter exact rollback, and deletion is explicitly reported unverified when its confirming list request fails.

## 2026-09-12 — Codex — batch Dashboard evidence refreshes after the high-concurrency package wave

- **Goal, hypothesis, environment, and isolation:** stop Dashboard refresh fan-out from exhausting GitHub's installation API quota while preserving the complete 151,852-entry inventory and its evidence-gated RPM/SRPM links. The hypothesis is that collecting every pull request, workflow run, and retained build/publication artifact after every individual Package CI completion is redundant during a 50-runner wave; one complete hourly snapshot plus relevant protected-main pushes and manual requests preserves eventual freshness without the API storm. Work is isolated on branch `infra/throttle-dashboard-api` at `/Users/askycn/Documents/Codex/2026-08-08/skill-creator-5-skill-apache-2/work/sep12/dashboard-throttle`, based on protected `main` `54117c10438dcea2b6e2668f5b1ce3a711669358`, on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.6.2 / Darwin 25.6 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 94 GiB free disk. The package target remains openEuler 24.03 LTS SP3 `riscv64`/RVA23; no Runner host, package source, RPM, QEMU image, supplemental repository object, or public Pages deployment is changed by local validation.
- **Observed failure and repair contract:** Package CI, Dashboard, and tracking runs completed their substantive build or generation work, but high-frequency completion callbacks caused `actions/configure-pages` and the Package CI state recorder to receive HTTP 403 installation-rate-limit responses. These are GitHub control-plane failures and do not convert successful package jobs into publication evidence. The Dashboard workflow therefore removes the per-workflow-completion trigger and defines a **batched Dashboard refresh** as one complete repository-wide evidence collection: relevant protected-main pushes still refresh immediately, a scheduled run executes hourly at minute 17, and `workflow_dispatch` remains available. Concurrency remains non-cancelling so an accepted snapshot is not replaced midway. The evidence collector, status precedence, schema, full inventory, and immutable-generation link requirements are unchanged; a just-completed run may lag by up to one hourly interval and missing retained evidence continues to produce a weaker status rather than a fabricated link.
- **Cleanup, validation, and next action:** the authoritative evidence round began with `make clean`; `make validate`, all 264 repository tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, the focused seven-test Dashboard suite, Actionlint, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. Dashboard regeneration retained exactly 151,852 inventory entries and left no generated-file diff. An initial ad hoc `python3 -m unittest -v scripts.tests.test_repair_dashboard` invocation lacked the test helper import path and failed before running a test; invoking the repository test file directly produced the authoritative 7/7 pass. The authenticated local credential guard passed over 2,840 paths before commit, push, and PR creation. Exact leases bound protected main `54117c10438dcea2b6e2668f5b1ce3a711669358` and pushed head `3a5469c2997d9aa05a66715470e3ced15c5d78b4` through ordinary non-force history to infrastructure PR #2050 with Auto-merge off. Its first `configure` CheckRun failed only because the GitHub installation API still returned HTTP 403 during the mandatory Auto-merge readback; exact-head Package CI remains active. After the rate window recovers, require terminal hosted checks before merge, then verify one scheduled snapshot and public Pages readback before retaining the hourly evidence boundary for continued package work.
